of list (L) are in
J
.∎

Theorem 4.1.9
.

The set of generators of
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
formed by

x^{e_{12}e_{12}}y^{e_{12}e},\,x^{e_{12}e_{12}}y^{e_{12}e_{21}}-y^{e_{12}e_{12}}x^{e_{12}e_{21}},\,[x^{gg},y^{gg}],\,[x^{gg},y^{e_{12}e_{12}}]

with
e:=e_{12}
if
k=2
and
e:=e_{31}
if
k\geq 3
, is minimal.

Proof.

Let us show first that the
U
-polynomials
[x^{gg},y^{gg}]
and
[x^{gg},y^{e_{12}e_{12}}]
cannot be generated by nor help in the generation of the other three identities. Suppose
f\in P_{2}^{U}
is of the form
f=f_{gg}+f_{ge_{12}}+f_{e_{12}e_{12}}
with
f_{gg}\in\langle[x^{gg},y^{gg}]\rangle_{T_{U}}
,
f_{ge_{12}}\in\langle[x^{gg},y^{e_{12}e_{12}}]\rangle_{T_{U}}
and
f_{e_{12}e_{12}}
and
f
in the ideal generated by the remaining two
U
-identities. Then by substitution (see
3.5.1
,
3.5.2
), since
\varphi_{gg}\varphi_{e_{12}e_{12}}=0
while
\varphi_{gg}^{2}=\varphi_{gg}
and
\varphi_{e_{12}e_{12}}^{2}=\varphi_{e_{12}e_{12}}
, by Formula (
2.3.2
) we get
0=f(x^{gg},y^{gg})=f_{gg}
and
0=f(x^{gg},y^{e_{12}e_{12}})=f_{ge_{12}}
, forcing
f=f_{e_{12}e_{12}}
. Analogously, if
f\in\langle[x^{gg},y^{gg}]\rangle_{T_{U}}
then
f_{ge_{12}}=0=f_{e_{12}e_{12}}
,
f=f_{gg}
, and if
f\in\langle[x^{gg},y^{e_{12}e_{12}}]\rangle_{T_{U}}
then
f_{gg}=0=f_{e_{12}e_{12}}
,
f=f_{ge_{12}}
.

Therefore it remains to show that
x^{e_{12}e_{12}}y^{e_{12}e}
is not in the
T_{U}
-ideal generated by
x^{e_{12}e_{12}}y^{e_{12}e_{21}}-y^{e_{12}e_{12}}x^{e_{12}e_{21}}
and vice versa. To this end, we first show the general form of multilinear
U
-polynomials in variables
x_{1},x_{2}
belonging to the
T_{U}
-ideal of a multilinear
U
-polynomial
f
in variables
x_{1},x_{2}
and without
g
-exponents. By Formula (
2.3.2
), any
U
-polynomial in
\langle f\rangle_{T_{U}}
is of the form
G:=\sum_{i=1}^{m}g_{i}f(p_{i1},p_{i2})^{u_{i}}g^{\prime}_{i}
, with the
i
th substitution
L
-endomorphism sending, for
j\in\{1,2\}
,
x_{j}
to
p_{ij}:=\sum_{a,b\in\mathcal{S}\text{ or }a=g=b}(\alpha^{ab}_{ij}x_{1}^{ab}+\beta^{ab}_{ij}x_{2}^{ab})+q_{ij}
, with
\alpha^{ab}_{ij},\beta^{ab}_{ij}\in F
and
q_{ij}\in\FU
such that
\deg q_{ij}>1
. Write

f=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2};r,s,t,u\in\mathcal{S}}\alpha_{rstu}^{\sigma}\,x_{\sigma(1)}^{rs}x_{\sigma(2)}^{tu}

with
\alpha_{rstu}^{\sigma}\in F
.
Then
f(p_{i1},p_{i2})=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2};a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{S}}\alpha_{abcd}^{\sigma,i}f(x_{\sigma(1)}^{ab},x_{\sigma(2)}^{cd})+q_{i}
, where
\alpha_{abcd}^{\sigma,i}\in F
and
q_{i}\in\FU
is of degree greater than
2
or not multilinear, and

\displaystyle G=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}g_{i}\left(\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2};a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{S}}\alpha_{abcd}^{\sigma,i}f(x_{\sigma(1)}^{ab},x_{\sigma(2)}^{cd})+q_{i}\right)^{u_{i}}g^{\prime}_{i}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq m;\sigma\in S_{2};a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{S}}\alpha_{abcd}^{\sigma,i}g_{i}f(x_{\sigma(1)}^{ab},x_{\sigma(2)}^{cd})^{u_{i}}g^{\prime}_{i}+g_{i}q_{i}^{u_{i}}g^{\prime}_{i}.

Now, due to the grading of
\FU
and the fact that
g_{i}q_{i}^{u_{i}}g^{\prime}_{i}
is of degree greater than
2
or not multilinear, when
G
is multilinear in
x_{1},x_{2}
it must equal the sum of its multilinear terms in
x_{1},x_{2}
(the rest of terms must cancel out). Therefore, reordering if necessary, there are some
1\leq i\leq m^{\prime}
such that
g_{i}=1=g^{\prime}_{i}
and

G=\sum_{1\leq i\leq m^{\prime};\sigma\in S_{2};a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{S}}\alpha_{abcd}^{\sigma,i}f(x_{\sigma(1)}^{ab},x_{\sigma(2)}^{cd})^{u_{i}}=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2};a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{S}}f(x_{\sigma(1)}^{ab},x_{\sigma(2)}^{cd})^{u_{abcd}^{\sigma}}

(with
u_{abcd}^{\sigma}:=\sum_{1\leq i\leq m^{\prime}}\alpha_{abcd}^{\sigma,i}u_{i}
).
Hence, any multilinear element of
\langle f\rangle_{T_{U}}
in
x_{1},x_{2}
is of the form

F(x_{1},x_{2}):=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2};a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{S};u\in U(L)^{\op}}\alpha_{a,b,c,d,u}^{\sigma}f(x_{\sigma(1)}^{ab},x_{\sigma(2)}^{cd})^{u},

where
\alpha_{a,b,c,d,u}^{\sigma}\in F
and only a finite number of these coefficients is nonzero.
Now put

f_{1}(x,y):=x^{e_{12}e_{12}}y^{e_{12}e}\text{ and }f_{2}(x,y):=x^{e_{12}e_{12}}y^{e_{12}e_{21}}-y^{e_{12}e_{12}}x^{e_{12}e_{21}}

in
\FU
; then all
U
-polynomials in
\langle f_{1}\rangle_{T_{U}}
(resp.
\langle f_{2}\rangle_{T_{U}}
) are of the form
F_{1}
(resp.
F_{2}
).

Let us show that
f_{1}\not\in\langle f_{2}\rangle_{T_{U}}
. If
f_{1}\in\langle f_{2}\rangle_{T_{U}}
, then for some
F_{2}
we have

f_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})=F_{2}(x_{1},x_{2})=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2};a,b\in\mathcal{S};u\in U(L)^{\op}}\!\!\!\!\alpha_{a,b,u}^{\sigma}(x_{\sigma(1)}^{ae_{12}}x_{\sigma(2)}^{be_{21}}-x_{\sigma(2)}^{be_{12}}x_{\sigma(1)}^{ae_{21}})^{u},

which is a skew-symmetric
U
-polynomial, so we have
f_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})=F_{2}(x_{1},x_{2})=-F_{2}(x_{2},x_{1})=-f_{1}(x_{2},x_{1})
, a contradiction since
f_{1}(x_{1},x_{2}),f_{1}(x_{2},x_{1})
are different elements of the basis of
\FU
.

To show that
f_{2}\not\in\langle f_{1}\rangle_{T_{U}}
we consider the
(L,U)
-algebra
\Mat_{2}(F)\otimes_{F}M_{k}(F)
with Hopf
U(L)
-action given by
(a\otimes b)^{u}:=a\otimes b^{u}
for
u\in U(L)^{\op}
,
a\in\Mat_{2}(F)
,
b\in M_{k}(F)
.
The evaluation at
u_{1},u_{2}\in M_{2}(F)\otimes M_{k}(F)
of a
U
-polynomial
F_{1}
takes the form

F_{1}(u_{1},u_{2})=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2};a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{S}\text{ or }c=g=d}\!\!\!\!\alpha_{a,b,c,d}^{\sigma}(u_{\sigma(1)}^{ae_{12}}u_{\sigma(2)}^{be})^{cd}.

On the one hand, we have

\displaystyle f_{2}(e_{12}\otimes e_{12},e_{21}\otimes e_{12})=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(e_{12}\otimes e_{12})^{e_{12}e_{12}}(e_{21}\otimes e_{12})^{e_{12}e_{21}}-(e_{21}\otimes e_{12})^{e_{12}e_{12}}(e_{12}\otimes e_{12})^{e_{12}e_{21}}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(e_{12}\otimes e_{12}^{e_{12}e_{12}})(e_{21}\otimes e_{12}^{e_{12}e_{21}})-(e_{21}\otimes e_{12}^{e_{12}e_{12}})(e_{12}\otimes e_{12}^{e_{12}e_{21}})=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(e_{12}\otimes e_{12})(e_{21}\otimes e_{21})-(e_{21}\otimes e_{12})(e_{12}\otimes e_{21})=e_{11}\otimes e_{11}-e_{22}\otimes e_{11}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{1}\otimes e_{11}\neq 0.

On the other hand, we get

\displaystyle F_{1}(e_{12}\otimes e_{12},e_{21}\otimes e_{12})=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2};a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{S}\text{ or }c=g=d}\!\!\!\!\alpha_{a,b,c,d}^{\sigma}((e_{\sigma(1)\sigma(2)}\otimes e_{12})^{ae_{12}}(e_{\sigma(2)\sigma(1)}\otimes e_{12})^{be})^{cd}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2};a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{S}\text{ or }c=g=d}\!\!\!\!\alpha_{a,b,c,d}^{\sigma}(e_{\sigma(1)\sigma(2)}\otimes e_{12}^{ae_{12}})(e_{\sigma(2)\sigma(1)}\otimes e_{12}^{be})^{cd}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2};c,d\in\mathcal{S}\text{ or }c=g=d}\!\!\!\!\alpha_{e_{12},e_{12},c,d}^{\sigma}((e_{\sigma(1)\sigma(2)}\otimes e_{12})(e_{\sigma(2)\sigma(1)}\otimes e))^{cd}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2};c,d\in\mathcal{S}\text{ or }c=g=d}\!\!\!\!\alpha_{e_{12},e_{12},c,d}^{\sigma}(e_{\sigma(1)\sigma(1)}\otimes e_{12}e)^{cd}=0,

as
e_{12}e_{12}=0=e_{12}e_{31}
.
Since
f_{2}(e_{12}\otimes e_{12},e_{21}\otimes e_{12})\neq F_{1}(e_{12}\otimes e_{12},e_{21}\otimes e_{12})
for any
F_{1}\in\langle f_{1}\rangle_{T_{U}}
, we conclude that
f_{2}\neq F_{1}
for any
F_{1}\in\langle f_{1}\rangle_{T_{U}}
, hence
f_{2}\not\in\langle f_{1}\rangle_{T_{U}}
.
∎

Remark 4.1.10

(
Generators of
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
in the unital case
)
.

In this remark we consider
M_{k}(F)
in the variety of unital associative
(L,U)
-algebras and take
\FU
to be unital. We have to take into account the
L
-endomorphisms of the form
x_{i}\mapsto\lambda
for fixed
i
and
\lambda\in F\cdot 1
, which allow to reduce the degree of
U
-polynomials with factors of the form
x_{i}^{gg}
, since
\varphi_{gg}=1-s
with
s:=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\varphi_{aa}
in
U
implies
\lambda^{gg}=\lambda
in
\FU
, as
s
is generated by derivations and
\lambda\in F\cdot 1
. These
reducing substitutions
do not affect the result of Theorem
4.1.9
, since they amount to the fact of allowing substitutions with
U
-polynomials with nonzero constant terms in the general expression given by Formula (
2.3.2
), and since in the bulk of the proof of Theorem
4.1.9
we only have to consider
U
-polynomials
f
with no
g
-exponents, such constant terms get killed by the derivations.

On the other hand, the reducing substitutions allow to show that
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
is principal as a
T_{U}
-ideal, at the expense of using three variables, for it is generated by the
U
-identity

x^{g}y^{e_{12}}z^{e}+x^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}z^{g}-y^{e_{12}}x^{e_{21}}z^{g}+[x^{g},z^{g}]y^{g}+[x^{g},z^{e_{12}}]y^{g}

(we get
y^{e_{12}}z^{e}
from
x\mapsto 1
,
x^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}-y^{e_{12}}x^{e_{21}}
from
z\mapsto 1
, and
[x^{g},z^{g}],[x^{g},z^{e_{12}}]
from
y\mapsto 1
then
z\mapsto z^{g}
and
z\mapsto z^{e_{12}}
, respectively).

4.2.
(L,U)
-identities of
M_{k}(F)

Theorem 4.2.1
.

The
T_{L,U}
-ideal of
(L,U)
-identities of
M_{k}(F)
is generated by the following identities:

(1)

[x^{gg},y^{gg}]
,

(2)

[x^{gg},y^{e_{12}e_{12}}]
,

(3)

x^{e_{12}e_{12}}y^{e_{12}e_{21}}-y^{e_{12}e_{12}}x^{e_{12}e_{21}}
,

(4)

x^{e_{12}e_{12}}y^{e_{12}e_{31}}
if
k\geq 3
.

Moreover
\I^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
is principal, generated as a
T_{L,U}
-ideal by the sum of identities (1)-(4).

Proof.

The first assertion is a direct consequence of Theorem
4.1.9
, which provides generators of
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
and hence of
\I^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
, with the caveat that if
k=2
, the
(L,U)
-polynomial
x^{e_{12}e_{12}}y^{e_{12}e_{12}}
is redundant because it is
0
in
\FLU
: we can write

\large x^{e_{12}e_{12}}y^{e_{12}e_{12}}=\frac{1}{4}x^{(e_{12}e_{21})E_{12}^{2}}y^{(e_{12}e_{21})E_{12}^{2}}=\left(\frac{1}{24}x^{e_{12}e_{21}}y^{e_{12}e_{21}}\right)^{E_{12}^{4}}=0

because
E_{12}^{3}=0
.

The second assertion is a consequence of the primitive element lemma (
2.2.2
): By the bracket formula (
3.3.2
), since
H_{1},\ldots,H_{k-1}\in U^{\op}
are derivations, for
a,b,c,d\in S
or
a=g=b
or
c=g=d
, with
b,d
weight vectors of
M_{k}(F)
for the adjoint action of
L
of respective weights
\alpha_{b},\alpha_{d}
, we get

(x^{ab}y^{cd})^{H_{i}}=x^{a[h_{i},b]}y^{cd}+x^{ab}y^{c[h_{i},d]}=(\alpha_{b}(h_{i})+\alpha_{d}(h_{i}))x^{ab}y^{cd},

which shows that
x^{ab}y^{cd}
is a weight vector of
\FLU
for the
L
-action, of weight
\alpha_{b}+\alpha_{d}
. Now, the elements
g,e_{12},e_{21},e_{31}\in M_{k}(F)
are weight vectors of
M_{k}(F)
for the adjoint action of
L
, with respective weights
0,\alpha,-\alpha,\beta
.
Hence the
(L,U)
-polynomials (1)-(4) in the statement are weight vectors of
\FLU
, with (2),(4) having different nonzero weights and (1),(3) having zero weight. By Lemma
2.2.2
we have
\langle(1)+(2)+(3)+(4)\rangle_{T_{L,U}}=\langle(2),(4),(1)+(3)\rangle_{T_{L,U}}
. In addition, applying the substitution
x\mapsto x^{e_{12}e_{12}}
to
(1)+(3)
, as
\varphi_{e_{12}e_{12}}\varphi_{gg}=0
and
\varphi_{e_{12}e_{12}}^{2}=\varphi_{e_{12}e_{12}}
, we get
(3)
as a consequence of
(1)+(3)
(hence also
(1)
, by subtraction). Therefore
\langle(1)+(3)\rangle_{T_{L,U}}=\langle(1),(3)\rangle_{T_{L,U}}
and the result is proven.
∎

Open Problem 4.2.2

(
Minimality of the set of generators
)
.

Is the set of generators of
\I^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
appearing in Theorem
4.2.1
minimal? We can reason similarly to the proof of Theorem
4.1.9
to find that (1),(2) are independent of themselves and of the rest, and that (3) is independent of (4); but the proof of (4) being independent of (3) now fails because we don’t know a basis of
\FLU
. So either (1)-(3) or (1)-(4) is a minimal set of generators. It could even happen that (4) is
0
in
\FLU
(hence redundant), but this we are not able to prove nor disprove at the moment, for we have found neither an expression of (4) in terms of the elements of
\ILU
, nor an
(L,U)
-algebra for which (4) is not an
(L,U)
-identity. Note that (1)-(3) are not
0
in
\FLU
, since there are nonzero evaluations for them from the
(L,U)
-algebra
M_{2}(F)\otimes_{F}M_{k}(F)
as in the proof of
4.1.9
(respectively, with elements
x:=e_{12}\otimes g,y:=e_{21}\otimes g
,
x:=e_{12}\otimes g,y:=e_{21}\otimes e_{12}
, and
x:=e_{12}\otimes e_{12},y:=e_{21}\otimes e_{12}
).

4.3.
L
-identities of
M_{k}(F)

Theorem 4.3.1
.

For
k\geq 2
let
L
be the Lie algebra of all derivations of
M_{k}(F)
,
c_{p,k}
for
2\leq p<k
be its
p
th Casimir element (given in (
3.4.3
)),
\lambda_{p,k}
the eigenvalue of
c_{p,k}
for
\Ad_{k}
(given in Lemma
3.4.4
), and denote
z_{p,k}:=c_{p,k}-\lambda_{p,k}
and
z:=e_{12}^{3}+e_{12}z_{2,k}+\cdots+e_{1k}z_{k,k}
. Let
\rho_{ab}
be a valid assignment of preimages of
\varphi_{ab}
in
U(L)^{\op}
(as in Proposition
3.4.2
).
The
T_{L}
-ideal of differential identities of
M_{k}(F)
is generated by the following
L
-polynomials:

(1)

x^{z}
,

(2)

[x^{\rho_{gg}},y^{\rho_{gg}+\rho_{e_{12}e_{12}}}]+x^{\rho_{e_{12}e_{12}}}y^{\rho_{e_{12}(e_{21}+e_{31})}}-y^{\rho_{e_{12}e_{12}}}x^{\rho_{e_{12}e_{21}}}
,

the
\rho_{e_{12}e_{31}}
exponent appearing only if
k\geq 3
.

Moreover,
c_{n}^{L}(M_{k}(F))=k^{2(n+1)}-(k^{2}-1)(n+1).

Proof.

The generators arise from Lemma
2.5.1
(1), Formula (
2.3.1
) together with Corollary
3.4.6
(which provide the first identity as generator of
\ILU
), and Theorem
4.2.1
(which gives the generator of
\I^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
). The
L
-codimensions formula arises from Lemmas
2.5.1
(2),
4.1.4
(which gives
c_{n}^{U}(M_{2}(F))
) and
4.1.6
(which gives
c_{n}^{U}(M_{k}(F))
for
k>2
).
∎

From
c_{n}^{L}(M_{k}(F))=k^{2(n+1)}-(k^{2}-1)(n+1)
we immediately get:

Corollary 4.3.2
.

(1)

\exp^{L}(M_{k}(F))=k^{2}.

(2)

The generating function of the differential codimensions,
C_{k}^{L}(x):=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c_{n}^{L}(M_{k}(F))x^{n}
, is rational:

C_{k}^{L}(x)=\frac{k^{2}}{1-k^{2}x}-\frac{k^{2}-1}{(1-x)^{2}}.

5.
Growth of differential codimensions of
M_{k}(F)

In this section, we shall prove that
\V^{L}(M_{k}(F))
,
k\geq 2
, is a variety of almost polynomial growth. To this end it is enough to work with
\V^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
and
U
-polynomials, by Propositions
2.5.5
and
2.5.1
(2). Recall that notation
x^{b}
with
x\in\FU
,
b\in\mathcal{S}
is shorthand for the element
x^{\varphi_{ab}}\in\FU
, for a fixed and elided first exponent index
a\in\mathcal{S}
which we will not explicitly mention in most of the next results (see
3.5.1
).

We start by proving some results on proper subvarieties of
\V^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
.

Lemma 5.1.1
.

For
a\in\mathcal{S}
,

(x^{ae_{12}}y^{ae_{21}})^{gg}\equiv\dfrac{1}{k}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}x^{ah_{i}}y^{ah_{i}}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-2}x^{ah_{i}}y^{ah_{i+1}}\right)\ (\md\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))).

Proof.

Since for any
x\in M_{k}(F)
and
a,b\in\mathcal{S}
,
x^{ab}=\mu^{x}_{a}b
(see
3.2.2
), for all
x,y\in M_{k}(F)
we have

(x^{ae_{12}}y^{ae_{21}})^{gg}=\mu^{x}_{a}\mu^{y}_{a}(e_{11})^{gg}=\dfrac{1}{k}\mu^{x}_{a}\mu^{y}_{a}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(k-i)h_{i}+g\right)^{gg}=\dfrac{1}{k}\mu^{x}_{a}\mu^{y}_{a}g

since
e_{11}=\dfrac{1}{k}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(k-i)h_{i}+g\right)
. On the other hand, since
g=\dfrac{1}{k}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}h_{i}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-2}h_{i}h_{i+1}\right)
, we have

\dfrac{1}{k}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}x^{ah_{i}}y^{ah_{i}}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-2}x^{ah_{i}}y^{ah_{i+1}}\right)=\dfrac{1}{k}\mu^{x}_{a}\mu^{y}_{a}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}h_{i}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-2}h_{i}h_{i+1}\right)=\dfrac{1}{k}\mu^{x}_{a}\mu^{y}_{a}g

and the proof is complete.
∎

We prove now a characterization of the proper subvarieties of
\V^{L,U}(M_{2}(F))
.

Proposition 5.1.2
.

Let
\textsc{V}=\V^{L,U}(A)
be a subvariety of
\V^{L,U}(M_{2}(F))
. Then
V
is a proper subvariety if and only if there exists
t\geq 2
such that
x_{1}^{e_{12}}x_{2}^{e_{21}}\cdots x_{t-1}^{e_{12}}x_{t}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(A)
.

Proof.

By the proof of Proposition
4.1.4
,
x_{1}^{e_{12}}x_{2}^{e_{21}}\cdots x_{t-1}^{e_{12}}x_{t}^{e_{21}}\notin\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))
for any
t\geq 1
, hence one implication is clear by Proposition
2.5.5
(3). Now, suppose that
V
is a proper subvariety of
\V^{L,U}(M_{2}(F))
. Then by Proposition
2.5.5
(3) there exists a
U
-polynomial
f\in\I^{U}(A)
such that
f\notin\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))
.
We may assume that
f
is a multilinear
U
-polynomial of degree
n
. Moreover, by Lemma
3.5.3
and Proposition
3.5.5
we may suppose that
f\in P^{U}_{r,n-r}\cap\I^{U}(A)
for some
0\leq r\leq n
. In order to simplify the notation let us identify
x_{r+i}^{a}
with
y_{i}^{a}
,
a\in\mathcal{S}
, for all
1\leq i\leq n-r
, so that variables
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}
correspond to exponents
g
and variables
y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-r}
correspond to exponents
a\in\mathcal{S}
. Since by Proposition
2.5.5
(3) we have
\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))\subseteq\I^{U}(A)
, we may reduce
f
modulo
\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))
. Thus, by the proof of Proposition
4.1.4
we may suppose that either

f=x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{n}^{g}

if
r=n
,

f=\alpha_{1}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{n-1}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{1}}+\alpha_{2}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{n-1}^{g}y_{1}^{e_{12}}+\alpha_{3}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{n-1}^{g}y_{1}^{e_{21}}

(5.1)

with
\alpha_{i}\in F
,
1\leq i\leq 3
, not all zero, if
r=n-1
,

\displaystyle f=

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}y_{3}^{e_{21}}y_{4}^{e_{12}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{e_{21}}y_{n-r}^{e_{12}}+\alpha_{2}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}y_{4}^{e_{21}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{e_{12}}y_{n-r}^{e_{21}}

(5.2)

\displaystyle+\alpha_{3}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{e_{12}}y_{2}^{e_{21}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{e_{12}}y_{n-r}^{e_{21}}+\alpha_{4}y_{1}^{g}\cdots y_{r}^{g}x_{1}^{e_{21}}x_{2}^{e_{12}}\cdots x_{n-r-1}^{e_{21}}x_{n-r}^{e_{12}}

with
\alpha_{i}\in F
,
1\leq i\leq 4
, not all zero, if
0\leq r\leq n-2
and
n-r
is even, or

\displaystyle f=

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}y_{3}^{e_{21}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{e_{12}}y_{n-r}^{e_{21}}+\alpha_{2}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{e_{21}}y_{n-r}^{e_{12}}

(5.3)

\displaystyle+\alpha_{3}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{e_{12}}y_{2}^{e_{21}}y_{3}^{e_{12}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{e_{21}}y_{n-r}^{e_{12}}+\alpha_{4}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}y_{3}^{e_{21}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{e_{12}}y_{n-r}^{e_{21}}

with
\alpha_{i}\in F
,
1\leq i\leq 4
, not all zero, if
0\leq r\leq n-2
and
n-r
is odd.

Suppose that
f
is as in (
5.2
). Let us assume that
\alpha_{1}\neq 0
; the remaining cases follow from similar arguments. Multiplying
f
on the right by
y_{n-r+1}^{e_{21}}
, since
y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))
by Lemma
4.1.3
, we get that

f^{\prime}:=\alpha_{1}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}y_{3}^{e_{21}}y_{4}^{e_{12}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{e_{21}}y_{n-r}^{e_{12}}y_{n-r+1}^{e_{21}}+\alpha_{4}y_{1}^{g}\cdots y_{r}^{g}x_{1}^{e_{21}}x_{2}^{e_{12}}\cdots x_{n-r-1}^{e_{21}}x_{n-r}^{e_{12}}y_{n-r+1}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(A).

Since
[x^{g},y^{e_{21}}],\ y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{h_{1}}+y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{21}},\ y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
) and
\alpha_{1}\neq 0
, if we multiply
f^{\prime}
on the left by
y_{n-r+2}^{e_{21}}
we get that

x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{n-r+2}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}y_{3}^{e_{21}}y_{4}^{e_{12}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{e_{21}}y_{n-r}^{e_{12}}y_{n-r+1}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(A)
.
We substitute variables to reorder them (we
rename
variables) and get

f^{\prime\prime}:=x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{21}}y_{3}^{e_{12}}y_{4}^{e_{21}}\cdots y_{n-r+1}^{e_{12}}y_{n-r+2}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(A).

Now for all
1\leq i\leq r
we substitute in
f^{\prime\prime}
the variable
x_{i}
with
y_{n-r+2+i}^{e_{12}}y_{n+2+i}^{e_{21}}
. By Lemma
5.1.1
and after renaming variables we obtain that

y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{2r+1}^{h_{1}}y_{2r+2}^{e_{21}}y_{2r+3}^{e_{12}}y_{2r+4}^{e_{21}}\cdots y_{n+r+1}^{e_{12}}y_{n+r+2}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(A).

Hence if we multiply the last
U
-polynomial on the left by
y_{n+r+3}^{h_{1}}
, by renaming the variables we obtain that

y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{2r+2}^{h_{1}}y_{2r+3}^{e_{21}}y_{2r+4}^{e_{12}}y_{2r+5}^{e_{21}}\cdots y_{n+r+2}^{e_{12}}y_{n+r+3}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(A)
. Now recall that
y_{1}^{e_{12}}y_{2}^{e_{21}}+y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}-y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{h_{1}},\ y_{1}^{c}y_{2}^{c}\in\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))
for
c\in\{e_{12},e_{21}\}
(Lemma
4.1.3
). Thus it follows that

y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}\cdots y_{n+r+2}^{e_{12}}y_{n+r+3}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(A)
.
Multiplying the last
U
-polynomial on the left by
y_{n+r+4}^{e_{12}}
and renaming variables we get that

y_{1}^{e_{12}}y_{2}^{e_{21}}\cdots y_{n+r+3}^{e_{12}}y_{n+r+4}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(A)

and we are done. One deals in a similar way with case (
5.3
).

Suppose now that
f=x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{n}^{g}
. If we substitute variable
x_{i}
with
y_{i}^{e_{12}}y_{i+n}^{e_{21}}
for all
1\leq i\leq n
, then by Lemma
5.1.1
and after renaming variables we get that
y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{2n-1}^{h_{1}}y_{2n}^{h_{1}}\in\I^{U}(A)
.
Since
y_{1}^{e_{12}}y_{2}^{e_{21}}+y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}-y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{h_{1}},\ y_{1}^{c}y_{2}^{c}\in\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))
for
c\in\{e_{12},e_{21}\}
, it follows that

y_{1}^{e_{12}}y_{2}^{e_{21}}\cdots y_{2n-1}^{e_{12}}y_{2n}^{e_{21}}+y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}\cdots y_{2n-1}^{e_{21}}y_{2n}^{e_{12}}\in\I^{U}(A)
.
Thus we obtain a
U
-polynomial of the form (
5.2
) with
r=0
and we are done.

Finally, suppose that
f
is as in (
5.1
). If we substitute in
f
the variable
x_{i}
with
y_{i+1}^{e_{12}}y_{i+n}^{e_{21}}
for all
1\leq i\leq n-1
then, by Lemma
5.1.1
, we obtain that

\alpha_{1}y_{2}^{h_{1}}y_{n+1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n}^{h_{1}}y_{2n-1}^{h_{1}}y_{1}^{h_{1}}+\alpha_{2}y_{2}^{h_{1}}y_{n+1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n}^{h_{1}}y_{2n-1}^{h_{1}}y_{1}^{e_{12}}+\alpha_{3}y_{2}^{h_{1}}y_{n+1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n}^{h_{1}}y_{2n-1}^{h_{1}}y_{1}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(A).

Thus, as we have done above, by reducing modulo
\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))
we obtain a
U
-polynomial of the form (
5.3
) with
r=0
. Now the proof is complete.
∎

Next, we prove some technical lemmas that lead to a similar characterization for the proper subvarieties of
\V^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
for
k\geq 3
.

Lemma 5.1.3
.

Let
A\in\V^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
for
k\geq 3
. If
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for some
1\leq i\leq k-1
and
s\geq 1
, then there exists
t\geq s
such that
x_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots x_{t}^{a_{t}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for all
a_{1},\dots,a_{t}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

Since
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))\subseteq\I^{U}(A)
by Proposition
2.5.5
(3), by the proof of Proposition
4.1.6
it is enough to prove that modulo
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
there exists
t\geq s
such that

x_{1}^{h_{j}}\cdots x_{t}^{h_{j}},\ x_{1}^{h_{j}}\cdots x_{t-1}^{h_{j}}x_{t}^{e_{jl}},\ x_{1}^{h_{k-1}}\cdots x_{t-1}^{h_{k-1}}x_{t}^{e_{km}},\ x_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots x_{t-1}^{h_{1}}x_{t}^{h_{2}}\in\I^{U}(A)

for all
1\leq j,m\leq k-1
,
1\leq l\leq k
,
l\neq j
.

Suppose that
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i}}\in\I^{U}(A)
with
1\leq i\leq k-2
. If we multiply
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i}}
on the right by
x_{s+1}^{e_{i+1,i}}x_{s+2}^{e_{i,i+1}}
, then since
x^{h_{i}}y^{e_{i+1,i}}+x^{h_{i+1}}y^{e_{i+1,i}},[x^{h_{i}},y^{h_{i+1}}]\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
by Lemma
4.1.3
, we get that

x_{1}^{h_{i+1}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i+1}}x_{s+1}^{e_{i+1,i}}x_{s+2}^{e_{i,i+1}}\in\I^{U}(A).

(5.4)

Moreover, since
x^{e_{i+2,i}}y^{h_{i}}-x^{h_{i+1}}y^{e_{i+2,i}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
) , by multiplying
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i}}
on the left by
x_{s+1}^{e_{i+2,i}}
, we get
x_{1}^{h_{i+1}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i+1}}x_{s+1}^{e_{i+2,i}}\in\I^{U}(A)
. Thus it follows that

x_{1}^{h_{i+1}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i+1}}x_{s+1}^{e_{i+2,i}}x_{s+2}^{e_{i,i+2}}\in\I^{U}(A).

(5.5)

Hence since
x^{e_{i+1,i}}y^{e_{i,i+1}}+x^{e_{i+2,i}}y^{i,i+2}-x^{h_{i+1}}y^{h_{i+1}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
), by (
5.4
) and (
5.5
) it follows that
x_{1}^{h_{i+1}}\cdots x_{s+2}^{h_{i+1}}\in\I^{U}(A)
. Therefore, by iteration, we obtain that
x_{1}^{h_{j}}\cdots x_{s+2}^{h_{j}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for all
i\leq j\leq k-1
.

Now if
2\leq i\leq k-1
, then we multiply
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i}}
on the left by
x_{s+1}^{e_{i-1,i}}
and on the right by
x_{s+2}^{e_{i,i-1}}
. Thus since
x^{e_{i-1,i}}y^{h_{i}}-x^{h_{i-1}}y^{e_{i-i,i}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
), we get

x_{1}^{h_{i-1}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i-1}}x_{s+1}^{e_{i-1,i}}x_{s+2}^{e_{i,i-1}}\in\I^{U}(A).

(5.6)

Now since
x^{h_{i}}y^{e_{i,i+1}}+x^{h_{i-1}}y^{e_{i,i+1}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
), by multiplying
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i}}
on the right by
x_{s+1}^{e_{i,i+1}}x_{s+2}^{e_{i+1,i}}
we have that

x_{1}^{h_{i-1}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i-1}}x_{s+1}^{e_{i,i+1}}x_{s+2}^{e_{i+1,i}}\in\I^{U}(A).

(5.7)

Thus since
x^{e_{i-1,i}}y^{e_{i,i-1}}+x^{e_{i,i+1}}y^{e_{i+1,i}}-x^{h_{i-1}}y^{h_{i-1}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
), from (
5.6
) and (
5.7
) we obtain that
x_{1}^{h_{i-1}}\cdots x_{s+2}^{h_{i-1}}\in\I^{U}(A)
. Hence, by iteration, we get that
x_{1}^{h_{j}}\cdots x_{s+2}^{h_{j}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for all
1\leq j\leq k-1
. Therefore it follows that
x_{1}^{h_{j}}\cdots x_{s+2}^{h_{j}}x_{s+3}^{e_{jl}},\ x_{1}^{h_{k-1}}\cdots x_{s+2}^{h_{k-1}}x_{s+3}^{e_{km}},\ x_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots x_{s+2}^{h_{1}}x_{s+3}^{h_{2}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for all
1\leq j,m\leq k-1
,
1\leq l\leq k
,
l\neq j
and the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 5.1.4
.

Let
A\in\V^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
, for
k\geq 3
. If
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s-1}^{h_{i}}x_{s}^{e_{ij}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for some
1\leq i\leq k-1
,
1\leq j\leq k
and
s\geq 1
, then there exists
t\geq s
such that
x_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots x_{t}^{a_{t}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for all
a_{1},\dots,a_{t}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))\subseteq\I^{U}(A)
(by Proposition
2.5.5
(3)). Then since
x^{a}y^{b}-y^{a}x^{b}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
for all
a,b\in\mathcal{S}
by Lemma
4.1.3
, modulo
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
, we can always reorder the indices
i_{l}
,
1\leq l\leq m
, in any
U
-monomial of type
x_{i_{1}}^{a_{1}}\cdots x_{i_{m}}^{a_{m}}
with
a_{1},\dots,a_{m}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Let now
1\leq i\leq k-1
and
1\leq j\leq k
such that
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s-1}^{h_{i}}x_{s}^{e_{ij}}\in\I^{U}(A)
,
s\geq 1
. Then we may assume that
j=i+1
: indeed if
j\neq i+1
, then
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s-1}^{h_{i}}x_{s}^{e_{ij}}x_{s+1}^{e_{j,i+1}}\in\I^{U}(A)
. Thus since
x^{h_{i}}y^{e_{i,i+1}}-x^{e_{ij}}y^{e_{j,i+1}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
), it follows that
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{i}}x_{s+1}^{e_{i,i+1}}\in\I^{U}(A)
.

Since
x_{1}^{e_{i+1,i}}x_{2}^{h_{i}}+x_{1}^{h_{i}}x_{2}^{e_{i+1,i}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
), if we multiply
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s-1}^{h_{i}}x_{s}^{e_{i,i+1}}\in\I^{U}(A)
on the left by
x_{s+1}^{e_{i+1,i}}
, then by reordering the index of the variables, as we may modulo
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
, we get
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s-1}^{h_{i}}x_{s}^{e_{i+1,i}}x_{s+1}^{e_{i,i+1}}\in\I^{U}(A)
. Moreover, we have also that
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s-1}^{h_{i}}x_{s}^{e_{i,i+1}}x_{s+1}^{e_{i+1,i}}\in\I^{U}(A)
. Thus since
x^{e_{i,i+1}}y^{e_{i+1,i}}+x^{e_{i+1,i}}y^{e_{i,i+1}}-x^{h_{i}}y^{h_{i}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
), we obtain that
x_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots x_{s+1}^{h_{i}}\in\I^{U}(A)
and by Lemma
5.1.3
we are done.
∎

Lemma 5.1.5
.

Let
A\in\V^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
, for
k\geq 3
. If
x_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots x_{s-1}^{h_{1}}x_{s}^{h_{2}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for some
1\leq i\leq k-1
and
s\geq 2
, then there exists
t\geq s
such that
x_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots x_{t}^{a_{t}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for all
a_{1},\dots,a_{t}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

If
x_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots x_{s-1}^{h_{1}}x_{s}^{h_{2}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for some
s\geq 2
, then also
x_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots x_{s-1}^{h_{1}}x_{s}^{h_{2}}x_{s+1}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(A)
. Since
[x^{h_{1}},y^{h_{2}}],\allowbreak x^{h_{1}}y^{e_{21}}+x^{h_{2}}y^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(by Lemma
4.1.3
) and
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))\subseteq\I^{U}(A)
(by Proposition
2.5.5
(3)), it follows that
x_{1}^{h_{2}}\cdots x_{s}^{h_{2}}x_{s+1}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(A)
and by Lemma
5.1.4
we are done.
∎

We are now in position to prove the following characterization of the proper subvarieties of
\V^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
for
k\geq 3
.

Proposition 5.1.6
.

Let
\textsc{V}=\V^{L,U}(A)
be a subvariety of
\V^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
,
k\geq 3
. Then
V
is a proper subvariety if and only if there exists
t\geq 1
such that
x_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots x_{t}^{a_{t}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for all
a_{1},\dots,a_{t}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

By the proof of Proposition
4.1.6
,
x_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots x_{t}^{a_{t}}\notin\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
for any
t\geq 1
and any
a_{1},\dots,a_{t}\in\mathcal{S}
, hence by Proposition
2.5.5
(3) one implication is clear. Let
\textsc{V}=\V^{L,U}(A)
be a proper subvariety of
\V^{L,U}(M_{k}(F))
,
k\geq 3
. Then by Proposition
2.5.5
(3) there exists a
U
-polynomial
f\in\I^{U}(A)
such that
f\notin\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
. We may assume that
f
is a multilinear
U
-polynomial of degree
n
. Moreover, by Lemma
3.5.3
and Proposition
3.5.5
we may suppose that
f\in P^{U}_{r,n-r}\cap\I^{U}(A)
where
0\leq r\leq n
.
In order to simplify the notation let us identify
x_{r+i}^{a}
with
y_{i}^{a}
,
a\in\mathcal{S}
, for all
1\leq i\leq n-r
, so that variables
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}
correspond to exponents
g
and variables
y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-r}
correspond to exponents
a\in\mathcal{S}
. Since by Proposition
2.5.5
(3) we have
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))\subseteq\I^{U}(A)
, we may reduce
f
modulo
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
. Thus, by the proof of Proposition
4.1.6
we may suppose that either

f=x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{n}^{g}

if
r=n
,

f=\sum_{1\leq i\leq k-1}\alpha_{i}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{n-1}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{i}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j,l\leq k\\
j\neq l\end{subarray}}\beta_{jl}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{n-1}^{g}y_{1}^{e_{jl}}

with
\alpha_{i},

\beta_{jl}
not all zero, if
r=n-1
, or

\displaystyle f=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq k-1}\alpha_{i}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots y_{n-r}^{h_{i}}+\beta x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{h_{1}}y_{n-r}^{h_{2}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq k-1\\
1\leq j\leq k\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\gamma_{ij}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{h_{i}}y_{n-r}^{e_{ij}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq l\leq k-1}\gamma_{kl}x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{r}^{g}y_{1}^{h_{k-1}}\cdots y_{n-r-1}^{h_{k-1}}y_{n-r}^{e_{kl}}

with
\alpha_{i},\beta,\gamma_{lj}
not all zero, if
0\leq r\leq n-1
.

Suppose first that
r=0
, i.e.

f=\sum_{1\leq i\leq k-1}\alpha_{i}y_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots y_{n}^{h_{i}}+\beta y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n-1}^{h_{1}}y_{n}^{h_{2}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq k-1\\
1\leq j\leq k\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\gamma_{ij}y_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots y_{n-1}^{h_{i}}y_{n}^{e_{ij}}+\sum_{1\leq l\leq k-1}\gamma_{kl}y_{1}^{h_{k-1}}\cdots y_{n-1}^{h_{k-1}}y_{n}^{e_{kl}},

with
\alpha_{i},\beta,\gamma_{lj}
not all zero. Let us assume that
\alpha_{k-1}\neq 0
. Since
y_{1}^{e_{1k}}y_{2}^{h_{i}},\ y_{1}^{e_{jl}}y_{2}^{e_{k,k-1}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
for all
1\leq i\leq k-2,

1\leq j\leq k
,
1\leq l\leq k-1
,
l\neq j
by Lemma
4.1.3
, by multiplying
f
on the left by
y_{n+1}^{e_{1k}}
and on the right by
y_{n+2}^{e_{k,k-1}}
we get

\alpha_{k-1}y_{n+1}^{e_{1k}}y_{1}^{h_{k-1}}\cdots y_{n}^{h_{k-1}}y_{n+2}^{e_{k,k-1}}+\gamma_{k-1,k}y_{n+1}^{e_{1k}}y_{1}^{h_{k}-1}\cdots y_{n-1}^{h_{k}-1}y_{n}^{e_{k-1k}}y_{n+2}^{e_{k,k-1}}\in\I^{U}(A)
. Since
y_{1}^{e_{1k}}y_{2}^{h_{k-1}}+y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{1k}},\ y_{1}^{e_{1k}}y_{2}^{e_{k-1,k}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
) and
\alpha_{k-1}\neq 0
and after renaming the variables, it follows that
y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n}^{h_{1}}y_{n+1}^{e_{1k}}y_{n+2}^{e_{k,k-1}}\in\I^{U}(A)
.
Moreover, since
y_{1}^{e_{1k}}y_{2}^{e_{k,k-1}}-y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{1,k-1}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
for
k\geq 3
(Lemma
4.1.3
), then
y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n+1}^{h_{1}}y_{n+2}^{e_{1,k-1}}\in\I^{U}(A)
and by Lemma
5.1.4
we are done. Thus we may assume that
\alpha_{k-1}=0
.
Now if
k\geq 4
and
\alpha_{k-2}\neq 0
, then we multiply
f
on the left by
y_{n+1}^{e_{1,k-1}}
and on the right by
y_{n+2}^{e_{k-1,k-2}}
and we obtain that
\alpha_{k-2}y_{n+1}^{e_{1,k-1}}y_{1}^{h_{k-2}}\cdots y_{n}^{h_{k-2}}y_{n+2}^{e_{k-1,k-2}}+\gamma_{k-2,k-1}y_{n+1}^{e_{1,k-1}}y_{1}^{h_{k}-2}\cdots y_{n-1}^{h_{k}-2}y_{n}^{e_{k-2,k-1}}y_{n+2}^{e_{k-1,k-2}}\in\I^{U}(A)
since
y_{1}^{e_{1,k-1}}y_{2}^{h_{i}},\ y_{1}^{e_{jl}}y_{2}^{e_{k-1,k-2}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
for all
1\leq i\leq k-3,

1\leq j\leq k
,
1\leq l\leq k
,
l\neq k-1
,
j\neq l
(Lemma
4.1.3
). As above since
y_{1}^{e_{1k-1}}y_{2}^{h_{k-2}}+y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{1k-1}},\ y_{1}^{e_{1k-1}}y_{2}^{e_{k-2,k-1}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
), and
\alpha_{k-2}\neq 0
, after renaming variables we get that
y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n+1}^{e_{1,k-1}}y_{n+2}^{e_{k-1,k-2}}\in\I^{U}(A)
. Moreover since
y_{1}^{e_{1,k-1}}y_{2}^{e_{k-1,k-2}}-y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{1,k-2}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
for
k\geq 4
(Lemma
4.1.3
), then
y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n+1}^{h_{1}}y_{n+2}^{e_{1,k-2}}\in\I^{U}(A)
and again by Lemma
5.1.4
we are done. Then we may assume that
\alpha_{k-2}=0
. Since if
k\geq 5
we can iterate the above procedure, we may assume that
\alpha_{2}=\cdots=\alpha_{k-1}=0
.

Now assume that
\alpha_{1}\neq 0
. By multiplying
f
on the left by
y_{n+1}^{e_{21}}
and on the right by
y_{n+2}^{e_{12}}
we get that
y_{n+1}^{e_{21}}y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n}^{h_{1}}y_{n+2}^{e_{12}}\in\I^{U}(A)
because
y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{h_{i}},\ y_{1}^{h_{2}}y_{2}^{e_{12}},\ y_{1}^{e_{lj}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
for all
2\leq i\leq k-1,

2\leq j\leq k
,
1\leq l\leq k-1
,
j\neq l
(Lemma
4.1.3
). Hence since
y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{h_{1}}-y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{21}},y_{1}^{e_{21}}y_{2}^{e_{12}}+y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{h_{2}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
), after renaming variables it follows that
y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n+1}^{h_{1}}y_{n+2}^{h_{2}}\in\I^{U}(A)
and by Lemma
5.1.5
we are done. So we may assume also that
\alpha_{1}=0
.

Let us suppose then that
\beta\neq 0
. By multiplying
f
on the left by
y_{n+1}^{e_{12}}
and on the right by
y_{n+2}^{e_{23}}
, as above we obtain that
y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n+1}^{h_{1}}y_{n+2}^{e_{13}}\in\I^{U}(A)
since
y_{1}^{e_{12}}y_{2}^{h_{i}},\ y_{1}^{e_{lj}}y_{2}^{e_{23}},\ y_{1}^{e_{12}}y_{2}^{h_{1}}+y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{12}},\ y_{1}^{e_{12}}y_{2}^{h_{2}}-y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{12}},\ y_{1}^{e_{12}}y_{2}^{e_{lj}},\ y_{1}^{e_{12}}y_{2}^{e_{23}}-y_{1}^{h_{1}}y_{2}^{e_{13}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
for all
3\leq i\leq k-1
,
1\leq j,j\leq k
,
j\neq 2
,
l\neq j
(Lemma
4.1.3
). Thus by Lemma
5.1.4
we are done. So we may assume that

f=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq k-1\\
1\leq j\leq k\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\gamma_{ij}y_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots y_{n-1}^{h_{i}}y_{n}^{e_{ij}}+\sum_{1\leq l\leq k-1}\gamma_{kl}y_{1}^{h_{k-1}}\cdots y_{n-1}^{h_{k-1}}y_{n}^{e_{kl}}

where at least one
\gamma_{ij}
,
1\leq i,j\leq k
,
i\neq j
, is not zero. Let
1\leq p,q\leq k
,
p\neq q
, such that
\gamma_{p,q}\neq 0
. Since
k\geq 3
, there exists
1\leq m\leq k
such that
m\neq p,q
. Since
y_{1}^{e_{mp}}y_{2}^{h_{i}},\ y_{1}^{e_{lj}}y_{2}^{e_{qp}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
,
1\leq i\leq k-1,\ 1\leq j,l\leq k,\ i\neq p,p-1,\ j\neq l,q
(Lemma
4.1.3
), if we multiply
f
on the left by
y_{n+1}^{e_{mp}}
and on the right by
y_{n+2}^{e_{qp}}
, then we get that either

\gamma_{pq}y_{n+1}^{e_{mp}}y_{1}^{h_{p}}\cdots y_{n-1}^{h_{p}}y_{n}^{e_{pq}}y_{n+2}^{e_{qp}}\in\I^{U}(A)

in case
p\neq k,k-1
or in case
(p,q)=(k-1,k)
, or

\gamma_{k-1,q}y_{n+1}^{e_{mp}}y_{1}^{h_{k-1}}\cdots y_{n-1}^{h_{k-1}}y_{n}^{e_{k-1,q}}y_{n+2}^{e_{q,p}}+\gamma_{kq}y_{n+1}^{e_{m,p}}y_{1}^{h_{k-1}}\cdots y_{n-1}^{h_{k-1}}y_{n}^{e_{kq}}y_{n+2}^{e_{qp}}\in\I^{U}(A)

in case
p=k-1
and
q\neq k
or in case
p=k
. Let us suppose first that
p\neq k
and
m=k
. Notice that from
m=k
it follows that
q\neq k
. Now since
y_{1}^{e_{kp}}y_{2}^{h_{p}}+y_{1}^{h_{k-1}}y_{2}^{e_{kp}},\ y_{1}^{e_{k,k-1}}y_{2}^{e_{kq}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(Lemma
4.1.3
) and
\gamma_{pq}\neq 0
, after renaming we have that
y_{1}^{h_{k-1}}\cdots y_{n-1}^{h_{k-1}}y_{n}^{e_{kp}}y_{n+1}^{e_{pq}}y_{n+2}^{e_{qp}}\in\I^{U}(A)
. Thus since
y_{1}^{e_{ki}}y_{2}^{e_{ij}}+y_{1}^{h_{k-1}}y_{2}^{e_{kj}}\in\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
with
(i,j)=(p,q)
or
(i,j)=(q,p)
(Lemma
4.1.3
), it follows that
y_{1}^{h_{k-1}}\cdots y_{n+1}^{h_{k-1}}y_{n+2}^{e_{k-1,p}}\in\I^{U}(A)
and by Lemma
5.1.4
we are done. The cases
p,m\neq k
and
p=k
follow with similar computations. Hence for
r=0
the proof is complete.

Suppose now that
r=n
, i.e.,
f=x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{n}^{g}\in\I^{U}(A)
. If for all
1\leq i\leq n
we substitute in
f
the variable
x_{i}
with
y_{i}^{e_{12}}y_{i+n}^{e_{21}}
, then by Lemma
5.1.1
we obtain a
U
-polynomial with exponents in
\{h_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k-1}
, and by reducing it modulo
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
(using the same strategy of Proposition
4.1.6
’s proof) we get that

\sum_{1\leq i\leq k-1}\alpha_{i}y_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots y_{n+r}^{h_{i}}+\beta y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n+r-1}^{h_{1}}y_{n+r}^{h_{2}}\in\I^{U}(A),

with
\alpha_{i},\beta
not all zero. Therefore, by repeating the same argument of the case
r=0
, we are done.

Finally, let us assume that
1\leq r\leq n-1
. By Lemma
5.1.1
, if we substitute
x_{i}
with
y_{i+n-r}^{e_{12}}y_{i+n}^{e_{21}}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
we obtain a
U
-polynomial with exponents in
\mathcal{S}
. Thus by reducing it modulo
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq k-1}\alpha^{\prime}_{i}y_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots y_{n+r}^{h_{i}}+\beta^{\prime}y_{1}^{h_{1}}\cdots y_{n+r-1}^{h_{1}}y_{n+r}^{h_{2}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq k-1\\
1\leq j\leq k\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\gamma^{\prime}_{ij}y_{1}^{h_{i}}\cdots y_{n+r-1}^{h_{i}}y_{n+r}^{e_{ij}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq l\leq k-1}\gamma^{\prime}_{kl}y_{1}^{h_{k-1}}\cdots y_{n+r-1}^{h_{k-1}}y_{n+r}^{e_{kl}}\in\I^{U}(A)

with
\alpha^{\prime}_{i},\beta^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}_{lj}
not all zero. Thus by repeating the same argument of the case
r=0
the proof is complete.
∎

We are now in position to prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 5.1.7
.

Let
L
be the Lie algebra of all derivations of
M_{k}(F)
,
k\geq 2
. Then the variety
\V^{L}(M_{k}(F))
has almost polynomial growth for all
k\geq 2
.

Proof.

Let
V
be a proper subvariety of
\V^{L}(M_{k}(F))
,
k\geq 2
. By Propositions
2.5.5
(2) and
2.5.1
(2) it suffices to show that
c_{n}^{U}(\textsc{V})
is polynomially bounded. Notice that as a consequence of Formula (
2
) it is enough to prove that there exists
N\geq 1
such that
c^{U}_{r,n-r}(\textsc{V})=0
whenever
n-r\geq N
: indeed, if such
N
exists, then we have that

c_{n}^{U}(\textsc{V})=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\binom{n}{n-r}(k^{2}-1)^{n-r}c^{U}_{r,n-r}(\textsc{V})\leq k^{2}\sum_{n-r<N}\binom{n}{n-r}(k^{2}-1)^{n-r}\leq k^{2}(k^{2}-1)^{N}\sum_{n-r<N}n^{N}\leq\alpha n^{N},

for
\alpha:=k^{2}(k^{2}-1)^{N}N
. Thus it follows that
\V^{L}(M_{k}(F))
has almost polynomial growth for all
k\geq 2
.

Suppose first that
k=2
. Then by Proposition
5.1.2
there exists
t\geq 2
such that
x_{1}^{e_{12}}x_{2}^{e_{21}}\cdots x_{t-1}^{e_{12}}x_{t}^{e_{21}}\in\I^{U}(\textsc{V})
. It follows that
x_{1}^{e_{12}}x_{2}^{e_{21}}\cdots x_{t-1}^{e_{12}}x_{t}^{e_{21}}x_{t+1}^{e_{12}},\ x_{1}^{e_{21}}x_{2}^{e_{12}}\cdots x_{t-1}^{e_{21}}x_{t}^{e_{12}}x_{t+1}^{e_{21}},\ x_{1}^{e_{21}}x_{2}^{e_{12}}\cdots x_{t+1}^{e_{21}}x_{t+2}^{e_{12}}\in\I^{U}(\textsc{V})
. Therefore since
[x^{g},y^{g}],[x^{g},y^{a}],x^{c}y^{h_{1}}+x^{h_{1}}y^{c}\in\I^{U}(\textsc{V})
for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
and
c\in\{e_{12},e_{21}\}
(Lemma
4.1.3
), we have that
c^{U}_{r,n-r}(\textsc{V})=0
if
n-r\geq t+2
.

Now let us assume that
k\geq 3
. By Proposition
5.1.6
there exists
t\geq 1
such that
x_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots x_{t}^{a_{t}}\in\I^{U}(A)
for all
a_{1},\dots,a_{t}\in\mathcal{S}
. Since
[x^{g},y^{g}],[x^{g},y^{a}]\in\I^{U}(\textsc{V})
for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
, it follows that
c^{U}_{r,n-r}(\textsc{V})=0
if
n-r\geq t
and we are done.
∎

6.
U
-cocharacter of
M_{k}(F)

In this section, we shall compute the
(n,r)
th
U
-cocharacter of
M_{k}(F)
for
k\geq 2
. Recall that notation
x^{b}
with
x\in\FU
,
b\in\mathcal{S}
is shorthand for the element
x^{\varphi_{ab}}\in\FU
, for a fixed and elided first exponent index
a\in\mathcal{S}
which we will not explicitly mention in most of the next results (see
3.5.1
). Similarly we write
P^{U}_{r,n-r}
instead of
P^{U}_{r,n-r,a}
(see
3.5.4
).

We start by proving some technical lemmas which give us a lower bound for the multiplicities
m_{\lambda,\mu}
of the
S_{r}\times S_{n-r}
-character

\chi_{r,n-r}^{U}(M_{k}(F))=\sum_{(\lambda,\mu)\vdash(r,n-r)}m_{\lambda,\mu}\ \chi_{\lambda}\otimes\chi_{\mu},

of
P^{U}_{r,n-r}(M_{k}(F))
, for
k\geq 2
(see
3.5.6
).
To this end recall that any irreducible left
S_{r}\times S_{n-r}
-module
W_{\lambda,\mu}\subseteq P_{r,n-r}^{U}
with character
\chi_{\lambda}\otimes\chi_{\mu}
can be generated as an
S_{r}\times S_{n-r}
-module by an element of the form
e_{T_{\lambda}}e_{T_{\mu}}f
, for some
f\in W_{\lambda,\mu}
and
some pair of Young tableaux
(T_{\lambda},T_{\mu})
of shape
\lambda\vdash r
and
\mu\vdash n-r
, respectively. Here
e_{T_{\nu}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in R_{T_{\nu}}\\
\tau\in C_{T_{\nu}}\end{subarray}}(\sgn\tau)\sigma\tau
stands for the symmetrizer corresponding to
some Young tableau
T_{\nu}
of shape
\nu\vdash p
, where
R_{T_{\nu}}
and
C_{T_{\nu}}
are the subgroups of
S_{p}
stabilizing the rows and columns of
T_{\nu}
, respectively.

Lemma 6.1.1
.

If
\lambda=(n-1)
and
\mu=(1)
, then
m_{\lambda,\mu}\geq k^{2}-1
.

Proof.

Let us consider the following tableaux

T_{\lambda}=\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr 1&2&\cdots&n-1\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\ ,\quad T_{\mu}=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr n\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\ .

Then, the
U
-polynomials

f_{b}(x,y)=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{n-1}y^{b},\ \ \ b\in\mathcal{S},

are obtained from the symmetrizers corresponding to the pair of tableaux
(T_{\lambda},T_{\mu})
by identifying all the elements in the row of
\lambda
. It is easily checked that
f_{b}(x,y)
,
b\in\mathcal{S}
, are not
U
-identities for
M_{k}(F)
.
Moreover, such
U
-polynomials are linearly independent modulo
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
. In fact, suppose that

\sum_{1\leq i\leq k-1}\alpha_{i}f_{h_{i}}(x,y)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l,j\leq k\\
l\neq j\end{subarray}}\beta_{lj}f_{e_{lj}}(x,y)\equiv 0\ (\md\ \I^{U}(M_{k}(F))).

The evaluation
x=g
and
y=\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}a
, gives

\sum_{1\leq i\leq k-1}\alpha_{i}h_{i}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq l,j\leq k\\
l\neq j\end{subarray}}\beta_{lj}e_{lj}=0.

Thus
\alpha_{i}=\beta_{lj}=0
,
1\leq i\leq k-1
,
1\leq l,j\leq k
,
l\neq j
. As a consequence the
U
-polynomials
f_{b}
,
b\in\mathcal{S}
, are linearly independent modulo
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
. For each
b\in\mathcal{S}
let
e_{\lambda,\mu,b}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n}):=e_{T_{\lambda}}e_{T_{\mu}}(x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{n-1}^{g}x_{n}^{b})
be the complete linearization of
f_{b}(x,y)
; it follows that the
k^{2}-1

U
-polynomials
e_{\lambda,\mu,b}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
are linearly independent modulo
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
. This implies that
m_{(n-1),(1)}\geq k^{2}-1
. ∎

Lemma 6.1.2
.

If
\lambda=(r)
and
\mu=(n-r)
,
0\leq r\leq n-2
, then
m_{\lambda,\mu}\geq k^{2}
.

Proof.

For
0\leq r\leq n-2
, let us consider the tableaux

T_{\lambda}=\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr 1&2&\cdots&r\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\ ,\quad T_{\mu}=\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr r+1&r+2&\cdots&n\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\ .

Suppose first
k=2
. We associate to the pair of tableaux
(T_{\lambda},T_{\mu})
the following
U
-polynomials:

\displaystyle f_{1}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{h_{1}}y^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}y^{e_{12}}\cdots y^{e_{21}}y^{e_{12}}}_{n-r},\quad f_{2}(x,y)=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{h_{1}}y^{e_{21}}y^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}\cdots y^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}}_{n-r},

\displaystyle f_{3}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}\cdots y^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}}_{n-r},\quad f_{4}(x,y)=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{e_{21}}y^{e_{12}}\cdots y^{e_{21}}y^{e_{12}}}_{n-r},

if
n-r
is even, or

\displaystyle f_{1}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{h_{1}}y^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}\cdots y^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}}_{n-r},\quad f_{2}(x,y)=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{h_{1}}y^{e_{21}}y^{e_{12}}\cdots y^{e_{21}}y^{e_{12}}}_{n-r},

\displaystyle f_{3}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}y^{e_{12}}\cdots y^{e_{21}}y^{e_{12}}}_{n-r},\quad f_{4}(x,y)=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{e_{21}}y^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}\cdots y^{e_{12}}y^{e_{21}}}_{n-r},

if
n-r
is odd. These
U
-polynomials are obtained from the symmetrizers corresponding to the pair of tableaux
(T_{\lambda},T_{\mu})
by identifying all the elements in the row of
T_{\lambda}
and
T_{\mu}
, respectively. Clearly they do not vanish on
M_{2}(F)
.
Also, they are linearly independent modulo
\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))
. In fact, suppose that

\alpha_{1}f_{1}(x,y)+\alpha_{2}f_{2}(x,y)+\alpha_{3}f_{3}(x,y)+\alpha_{4}f_{4}(x,y)\equiv 0\ (\md\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))).

Then if we evaluate
x=g
and
y=h_{1}+e_{12}+e_{21}
, we get either
\alpha_{1}e_{12}-\alpha_{2}e_{21}+\alpha_{3}e_{11}+\alpha_{4}e_{22}=0
if
n-r
is even, or
\alpha_{1}e_{11}-\alpha_{2}e_{22}+\alpha_{3}e_{12}+\alpha_{4}e_{21}=0
if
n-r
is odd. Thus
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}=\alpha_{4}=0
in both cases. This implies that
f_{i}(x,y)
,
1\leq i\leq 4
, are linearly independent modulo
\I^{U}(M_{2}(F))
. As in
6.1.1
, this implies that
m_{(r),(n-r)}\geq 4
, for
0\leq r\leq n-2
.

Now let
k>2
. Then we consider the following
U
-polynomial associated to the pair of tableaux
(T_{\lambda},T_{\mu})
:

\displaystyle f_{i,j}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{h_{i}}\cdots y^{h_{i}}y^{e_{ij}}}_{n-r},\quad f_{k,l}(x,y)=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{h_{k-1}}\cdots y^{h_{k-1}}y^{e_{kl}}}_{n-r},

\displaystyle g_{m}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{h_{m}}\cdots y^{h_{m}}}_{n-r},\quad p(x,y)=\underbrace{x^{g}\cdots x^{g}}_{r}\ \underbrace{y^{h_{1}}\cdots y^{h_{1}}y^{h_{2}}}_{n-r},

1\leq i,j,l\leq k
,
i\neq j
,
l\neq k
,
1\leq m\leq k-1
. These
U
-polynomials are obtained from the symmetrizers corresponding to the pair of tableaux
(T_{\lambda},T_{\mu})
by identifying all the elements in the row of
T_{\lambda}
and
T_{\mu}
, respectively. Also, it is clear that
f_{i,j}(x,y),

g_{m}(x,y)
,
p(x,y)
,
1\leq i,j\leq k
,
i\neq j
,
1\leq m\leq k-1
, are not
U
-identities of
M_{k}(F)
. Next, we shall prove that they are linearly independent modulo
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
. Suppose that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i,j\leq k\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\alpha_{i,j}f_{i,j}(x,y)+\sum_{1\leq m\leq k-1}\beta_{m}g_{m}(x,y)+\gamma p(x,y)\equiv 0\ (\md\ \I^{U}(M_{k}(F))).

If we evaluate
x=g
and
y=\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}a
, then we get

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq k-1\\
1\leq j\leq k\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\alpha_{i,j}e_{ij}+(-1)^{n-r-1}\sum_{1\leq j\leq k-1}\alpha_{kj}e_{kj}+\sum_{1\leq m\leq k-1}\beta_{m}h_{m}+\gamma e_{22}=0.

Thus it follows that
\alpha_{i,j}=\beta_{m}=\gamma=0
,
1\leq i,j\leq k
,
i\neq j
,
1\leq m\leq k-1
. Hence the
U
-polynomials
f_{i,j}(x,y),

g_{m}(x,y)
,
p(x,y)
,
1\leq i,j\leq k
,
i\neq j
,
1\leq m\leq k-1
, are linearly independent modulo
\I^{U}(M_{k}(F))
. Again, as in
6.1.1
, this implies that
m_{(r),(n-r)}\geq k^{2}
for
0\leq r\leq n-2
.
∎

At this point, we can prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 6.1.3
.

Let
n\geq 1
and
0\leq r\leq n
. The
(n,r)
th
U
-cocharacter of
M_{k}(F)
,
k\geq 2
, is

\chi^{U}_{(n;r)}(M_{k}(F))=\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\binom{n}{k}\,\chi_{(n)}\otimes\chi_{\emptyset}&\mbox{ if }r=n,\\
\displaystyle\binom{n}{k}(k^{2}-1)^{2}\,\chi_{(n-1)}\otimes\chi_{(1)}&\mbox{ if }r=n-1,\\
\displaystyle\binom{n}{k}(k^{2}-1)^{n-r}k^{2}\,\chi_{(r)}\otimes\chi_{(n-r)}&\mbox{ if }n-2\geq r\geq 0.\end{cases}

Proof.

By formula (
3.5.6
) we have
\chi^{U}_{(n;r)}(M_{k}(F))=\sum_{(\lambda,\mu)\vdash(r,n-r)}\binom{n}{k}(k^{2}-1)^{n-r}m_{\lambda,\mu}\ \chi_{\lambda}\otimes\chi_{\mu}
, so it is enough to compute the multiplicities
m_{\lambda,\mu}
of the
S_{r}\times S_{n-r}
character
\displaystyle\chi_{r,n-r}^{U}(M_{k}(F))
of
P^{U}_{r,n-r}(M_{k}(F))
. We show that

m_{\lambda,\mu}=\begin{cases}1&\mbox{ if }r=n\text{ and }(\lambda,\mu)=((n),\emptyset),\\
k^{2}-1&\mbox{ if }r=n-1\text{ and }(\lambda,\mu)=((n-1),(1)),\\
k^{2}&\mbox{ if }\ n-2\geq r\geq 0\text{ and }(\lambda,\mu)=((r),(n-r)),\\
0&\mbox{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

To this end we shall use that
c^{U}_{r,n-r}(M_{k}(F))=\deg\chi_{r,n-r}^{U}(M_{k}(F))=\sum_{(\lambda,\mu)\vdash(r,n-r)}m_{\lambda,\mu}\ d_{\lambda}d_{\mu}
, where
d_{\lambda}=\deg\chi_{\lambda}
and
d_{\mu}=\deg\chi_{\mu}
.

First notice that if
r=n
,
\lambda=(n)
and
\mu=\emptyset
, then
f(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=x_{1}^{g}\cdots x_{n}^{g}
is a
U
-polynomial associated to the pair of tableaux

T_{\lambda}=\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr 1&2&\cdots&n\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\,,\,\quad T_{\mu}=\emptyset

that is not an
U
-identity of
M_{k}(F)
. Thus
m_{(n),\emptyset}\geq 1
. By Propositions
4.1.4
and
4.1.6
we have that

1=c_{n,0}(M_{k}(F))\geq d_{(n)}d_{\emptyset}=1.

Hence
m_{(n),\emptyset}=1
.
Now assume that
r=n-1
. By Propositions
4.1.4
,
4.1.6
and Lemma
6.1.1
we get

k^{2}-1=c_{n-1,1}(M_{k}(F))\geq(k^{2}-1)d_{(n-1)}d_{(1)}=k^{2}-1.

Thus
m_{(n-1),(1)}=k^{2}-1
.
Finally, suppose
\lambda=(r)
and
\mu=(n-r)
,
0\leq r\leq n-2
. By Propositions
4.1.4
,
4.1.6
and Lemma
6.1.2
, it follows that

k^{2}=c^{U}_{r,n-r}(M_{k}(F))\geq k^{2}d_{(r)}d_{(n-r)}=k^{2}.

Thus
m_{(n-r),(r)}=k^{2}

and we are done.
∎

Acknowledgements:
We are grateful to Professor Alberto Elduque for pointing us to absolute irreducibility for the proof of Theorem
2.1.4
and for detecting a mistake in an earlier version of Lemma
2.1.6
.
